## GHSA-8cfw-pcwh-v63w · winter/wn-system-module

2026-08-20 · PHP · **HIGH** 8.4 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→TEMPLATE`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from an incomplete blocklist in the Twig security policy. Specifically, the previous patch (CVE-2024-54149) failed to account for how Laravel's database layer forwards method calls (e.g., Model::__call forwarding to Eloquent Builder, then to Query Builder). This allowed an attacker to bypass the sandbox by invoking blocked methods indirectly through these forwarding mechanisms, leading to data modification or arbitrary code execution.

### Impact

An authenticated attacker with the ability to inject Twig code could execute arbitrary PHP code, modify database records, or read sensitive files, leading to full system compromise.

### Key diff

```
--- a/modules/system/twig/SecurityPolicy.php
+++ b/modules/system/twig/SecurityPolicy.php
@@ -53,6 +53,190 @@ final class SecurityPolicy implements SecurityPolicyInterface
             'fireEvent',
             'fireSystemEvent',
         ],
+
+        // Prevent model data modification. Methods that forward to the query layer
+        // (increment, decrement, touch, getConnection, ...) are covered transitively by
+        // $blockedForwarders; only methods that physically live on the Model are listed here.
+        DbModel::class => [
+            'fill',
+            'forceFill',
+            'setAttribute',
+            'setRawAttributes',
+            'save',
+            'saveQuietly',
+            'saveOrFail',
+            'push',
+            'pushQuietly',
+            'update',
+            'updateQuietly',
+            'updateOrFail',
+            'delete',
+            'deleteQuietly',
+            'deleteOrFail',
+            'forceDelete',
+            'destroy',
+            'forceDestroy',
+            'restore',
+            'restoreQuietly',
+            'getQuery',
+            // Re-pointing the table/connection would allow reading arbitrary tables/databases
+            'setTable',
+            'setConnection',
+            'on',
+            'onWriteConnection',
+            'setKeyName',
+            'setKeyType',
+            'setIncrementing',
+            'setPerPage',
+            'setDateFormat',
+            'offsetSet',
+            'offsetUnset',
+            // Disabling mass-assignment / event protection, or executing callbacks
+            'unguard',
+            'reguard',
+            'unguarded',
+            'withoutEvents',
+            'withoutTouching',
+            'withoutTouchingOn',
+            // getConnectionResolver returns the DatabaseManager, whose __call proxies raw SQL
+            'getConnectionResolver',
+            'setConnectionResolver',
+            'unsetConnectionResolver',
+            'flushEventListeners',
+            'getEventDispatcher',
+            'setEventDispatcher',
+            'unsetEventDispatcher',
+        ],
+
+        EloquentBuilder::class => [
+            'forceDelete',
+            'create',
+            'createQuietly',
+            'forceCreate',
+            'forceCreateQuietly',
+            'firstOrCreate',
+            'createOrFirst',
+            'updateOrCreate',
+            'incrementOrCreate',
+            'fillAndInsert',
+            'fillAndInsertOrIgnore',
+            'fillAndInsertGetId',
+            'touch',
+            'update',
+            'delete',
+            'upsert',
+        ],
+
+        QueryBuilder::class => [
+            'insert',
+            'insertOrIgnore',
+            'insertGetId',
+            'insertUsing',
+            'insertOrIgnoreUsing',
+            'update',
+            'updateFrom',
+            'updateOrInsert',
+            'upsert',
+            'delete',
+            'truncate',
+            'increment',
+            'incrementEach',
+            'incrementQuietly',
+            'decrement',
+            'decrementEach',
+            'decrementQuietly',
+            // Re-pointing the table
+            'from',
+            'fromRaw',
+            'fromSub',
+            // Connection / raw SQL
+            'getConnection',
+            'toRawSql',
+            'selectRaw',
+            'whereRaw',
+            'orWhereRaw',
+            'havingRaw',
+            'orHavingRaw',
+            'orderByRaw',
+            'groupByRaw',
+            'joinSub',
+            'leftJoinSub',
+            'rightJoinSub',
+            'crossJoinSub',
+            'raw',
+            'rawValue',
+            'dd',
+            'dump',
+            'ddRawSql',
+            // callable-typed executors (string callables would execute)
+            'when',
+            'unless',
+            'each',
+            'eachById',
+            'chunk',
+            'chunkById',
+            'chunkByIdDesc',
+            'chunkMap',
+            'tap',
+            'pipe',
+        ],
+
+        Relation::class => [
+            'attach',
+            'detach',
+            'sync',
+            'syncWithPivotValues',
+            'syncWithoutDetaching',
+            'toggle',
+            'updateExistingPivot',
+            'save',
+            'saveQuietly',
+            'saveMany',
+            'saveManyQuietly',
+            'create',
+            'createQuietly',
+            'createMany',
+            'createManyQuietly',
+            'forceCreate',
+            'forceCreateQuietly',
+            'push',
+            'update',
+            'updateOrCreate',
+            'firstOrCreate',
+            'firstOrNew',
+            'createOrFirst'
```

### Fix summary

The patch significantly expands the blocklist in the Twig SecurityPolicy to cover a much wider range of classes and methods, including those involved in database operations (Eloquent Builder, Query Builder, Relations) and event dispatching. It explicitly addresses the forwarding behavior of the database layer by blocking methods on all relevant levels, preventing indirect invocation of dangerous functions. Additionally, it blocks methods related to dynamic code execution, event binding, and file operations within the CMS Controller.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 13 occurrences**
> Languages: Java, PHP, Python, JavaScript

## GHSA-p853-83gj-wjj3 · @nocobase/plugin-backups

2026-08-20 · JavaScript · **MODERATE** 6.7 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→COMMAND`

### Root cause

The application constructed shell commands by concatenating user-controlled input (like schema names) directly into the command string without proper escaping for shell arguments. This allowed an attacker to inject arbitrary shell commands.

### Impact

An attacker could execute arbitrary commands on the server where the NocoBase application is running, leading to full system compromise.

### Key diff

```
--- a/packages/plugins/@nocobase/plugin-backups/src/server/adapters/database.ts
+++ b/packages/plugins/@nocobase/plugin-backups/src/server/adapters/database.ts
-    const command = `${this.getBackupCommandName()} ${includeOption} ${excludeOption} -U ${username} -h ${host} ${port ? `-p ${port}` : ''} -F c -b --quote-all-identifiers ${schemaOption} -f ${filePath} ${database}`;
-    await run(command, this.getPasswordEnvVars(password));
+    const args = [
+      ...includeArgs,
+      ...excludeArgs,
+      ...this.getConnectionArgs(false),
+      '-F',
+      'c',
+      '-b',
+      '--quote-all_identifiers',
+      ...(backupSchema ? [`--schema=${backupSchema}`] : []),
+      '-f',
+      filePath,
+      database,
+    ];
+    await runSpawn(this.getBackupCommandName(), args, this.getPasswordEnvVars(password));
```

### Fix summary

The patch refactors command execution to use `spawn` with an array of arguments instead of `exec` with a single command string. This ensures that user-provided values are passed as distinct arguments to the external command, preventing shell metacharacters from being interpreted.

## GHSA-4xfr-4p46-gc6p · django-cms

2026-08-20 · Python · **MODERATE** 6.5 · Pattern: `IDOR→DATA_ACCESS`

### Root cause

The application failed to properly enforce permissions when copying plugins to the clipboard and when rendering object structures. Specifically, it did not check if the user had permission to access the *source* content (plugin or page) before allowing it to be copied or its structure to be viewed, leading to unauthorized data disclosure.

### Impact

An authenticated staff user could copy content from plugins they were not authorized to view into their clipboard, or view the plugin structure of pages they did not have permission to access, thereby exfiltrating sensitive information.

### Key diff

```
--- a/cms/admin/placeholderadmin.py
+++ b/cms/admin/placeholderadmin.py
@@ -533,11 +533,23 @@ def _copy_plugin_to_clipboard(self, request, target_placeholder):
             language=source_language,
         )
         old_plugins = [source_plugin] + list(source_plugin.get_descendants())
+        source_placeholder = source_plugin.placeholder
 
         if not self.has_copy_plugins_permission(request, old_plugins):
             message = _('You do not have permission to copy these plugins.')
             raise PermissionDenied(message)
 
+        # Check source-side permission as well: copying reads the source
+        # plugins, so a user without access to the source placeholder must not
+        # be able to exfiltrate its content into their clipboard.
+        if not source_placeholder.has_add_plugins_permission(request.user, old_plugins):
+            message = _('You do not have permission to copy these plugins.')
+            raise PermissionDenied(message)
+
+        if not source_placeholder.check_source(request.user):
+            message = _('You do not have permission to copy these plugins.')
+            raise PermissionDenied(message)
```

### Fix summary

The patch adds explicit permission checks for the source content (plugins and placeholders) during copy operations to the clipboard. It also introduces a page-view permission check for the `render_object_structure` endpoint to ensure users cannot view the structure of pages they are not authorized to see.

## GHSA-6x92-6vx4-5fwr · django-cms

2026-08-20 · Python · **MODERATE** 6.5 · Pattern: `MISSING_AUTHZ→RESOURCE`

### Root cause

The vulnerability had two main root causes: 1) The 'Duplicate Page' functionality did not perform an object-level permission check, allowing a staff user with 'add page' rights to duplicate and read the content of any page, bypassing per-page view restrictions and multi-site isolation. 2) The URL validation for relative URLs only checked character patterns, not the presence of a scheme, allowing values like 'javascript:alert(1)/x' to pass validation and potentially lead to XSS when rendered in an 'href'. Additionally, a delegated manager could escalate group permissions beyond their own rights due to the form not dropping unauthorized permission fields.

### Impact

An attacker could read the content of any page, including those with restricted access, by duplicating them. They could also potentially execute arbitrary JavaScript in a user's browser via crafted URLs, leading to Cross-Site Scripting (XSS). Furthermore, a delegated manager could escalate permissions for user groups.

### Key diff

```
--- a/cms/admin/forms.py
+++ b/cms/admin/forms.py
@@ -518,6 +525,22 @@ class DuplicatePageForm(AddPageForm):
         widget=forms.HiddenInput(),
     )
 
+    def clean_source(self):
+        source = self.cleaned_data.get("source")
+        if source and not user_can_view_page(self._user, source):
+            raise ValidationError(_("You do not have permission to copy this page."))
+        return source
 
 def url_is_locked(page_content: PageContent) -> bool:
     """Return whether ``page_content``'s URL is shared with a published
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces explicit object-level permission checks for duplicating pages, ensuring the user has 'view' permission for the source page. It also adds a scheme check to the URL validator to prevent malicious schemes in relative URLs. Finally, it modifies the permission form to drop any 'can_*' permission fields that the current user is not authorized to manage, preventing privilege escalation.

## GHSA-vgxm-h9gx-h9w7 · django-cms

2026-08-20 · Python · **MODERATE** 6.5 · Pattern: `MISSING_AUTHZ→RESOURCE`

### Root cause

The `render_object_structure` endpoint in django CMS did not perform the necessary page-view permission checks. This allowed authenticated staff users, who were otherwise restricted from viewing certain pages, to access the structural information (plugin details) of those restricted pages.

### Impact

An attacker with staff privileges could bypass page-level view restrictions and exfiltrate the content structure of pages they were not authorized to view, leading to information disclosure.

### Key diff

```
--- a/cms/views.py
+++ b/cms/views.py
@@ -255,6 +256,12 @@ def render_object_structure(request, content_type_id, object_id):
             content_type_obj = PageContent._base_manager.select_related("page").get(pk=object_id)
             request.current_page = content_type_obj.page
             # Enforce the same page-view permission as the edit and preview
-            # endpoints (which check it via render_page()). Without this, a
-            # staff user who cannot view a restricted page could still read its
-            # plugin structure through this endpoint.
+            # endpoints (which check it via render_page()). Without this,
+            # a staff user who cannot view a restricted page could still
+            # read its plugin structure through this endpoint.
+            if not user_can_view_page(request.user, content_type_obj.page):
+                raise Http404
         else:
             content_type_obj = content_type.get_object_for_this_type(pk=object_id)
     except ObjectDoesNotExist as err:
```

### Fix summary

The patch adds a call to `user_can_view_page` in the `render_object_structure` view. This ensures that the same page-view permissions enforced by other endpoints are also applied to this structure endpoint, preventing unauthorized access to page content structure.

## GHSA-p2ch-c2c3-4xm5 · winter/wn-backend-module

2026-08-20 · PHP · **MODERATE** 6.1 · Pattern: `CSRF→STATE_CHANGE`

### Root cause

The Winter CMS backend allowed AJAX handler names to be reachable as backend page actions. Specifically, the system did not properly enforce case sensitivity for action names, allowing attackers to craft URLs with mixed-case action names that would bypass CSRF protection mechanisms, which typically rely on exact action name matching.

### Impact

An attacker could trick an authenticated backend user into performing unintended actions via a crafted link or malicious website, leading to unauthorized state changes within the application.

### Key diff

```
--- a/modules/backend/classes/Controller.php
+++ b/modules/backend/classes/Controller.php
@@ -372,6 +372,13 @@ public function actionExists($name, $internal = false)
              * method (eg. "index_onemptylog" reaching index_onEmptyLog()).
              */
             if (strtolower($methodInfo->getName()) !== $methodInfo->getName()) {
                 return false;
             }
 
             $public = $methodInfo->isPublic();
--- a/modules/backend/classes/BackendController.php
+++ b/modules/backend/classes/BackendController.php
@@ -298,7 +298,7 @@ protected function findController($controller, $action, $inPath)
     protected function parseAction($actionName)
     {
         if (strpos($actionName, '-') !== false) {
-            return camel_case($actionName);
+            return snake_case(camel_case($actionName));
         }
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces strict case-sensitivity checks for action names in the `Controller.php` and `BackendController.php` files. It ensures that only lowercase action names are processed, preventing mixed-case variations from bypassing security checks and normalizing action names to snake_case before camel_casing them for method resolution.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 13 occurrences**
> Languages: PHP, JavaScript, Go
> → GHSA-ffw8-fwxp-h64w
> → GHSA-vvfw-4m39-fjqf
> → GHSA-8qm8-g55h-xmqr
> → GHSA-x2pw-9c38-cp2j
> → GHSA-c73c-x77g-854r
> → GHSA-wxw3-q3m9-c3jr
> → GHSA-pwx3-qcgw-vh7h
> → GHSA-v7j5-vc4m-723w
> → GHSA-xpr4-8vp6-c87j
> → GHSA-rgr9-r7mj-mf6x
> → GHSA-fm3f-ch8h-qw8q
> → GHSA-68jx-f42c-7599

## GHSA-9w56-46f6-3qhx · asteval

2026-08-20 · Python · **MODERATE** 5.5 · Pattern: `INSECURE_DEFAULT→CONFIG`

### Root cause

The `asteval` interpreter, designed for safe evaluation of mathematical expressions, allowed access to dangerous attributes like `numpy.ndarray.ctypes`. This permitted an attacker to bypass the sandbox restrictions and interact with native memory.

### Impact

An attacker could achieve arbitrary native memory read/write operations, effectively escaping the sandbox and potentially executing arbitrary code or accessing sensitive system resources.

### Key diff

```
--- a/asteval/astutils.py
+++ b/asteval/astutils.py
@@ -78,6 +73,8 @@ def formatter_field_name_split(x):
 
 # unsafe attributes for particular objects, by type
 UNSAFE_ATTRS_DTYPES = {str: ('format', 'format_map')}
+if HAS_NUMPY:
+    UNSAFE_ATTRS_DTYPES[numpy.ndarray] = ('ctypes', 'tofile', 'dump')
 
 # unsafe modules that may be exposed in other modules
 # but should be prevented from being accessed
```

### Fix summary

The patch explicitly adds `ctypes`, `tofile`, and `dump` to the list of unsafe attributes for `numpy.ndarray` objects, preventing their access within the `asteval` interpreter. It also removes some unnecessary error handling and reintroduces `formatter_field_name_split` directly from `_string`.

## GHSA-3277-h8g9-qj5f · winter/wn-backend-module

2026-08-20 · PHP · **MODERATE** 5.4 · Pattern: `IDOR→DATA_ACCESS`

### Root cause

The application allowed authenticated users to access and manipulate attachment metadata belonging to other users or models. When retrieving or sorting file records, the system only checked for the existence of a file ID in the POST request, without verifying if the file was actually associated with the current user's context or the specific model instance being edited. This allowed an attacker to supply an arbitrary `file_id` to interact with any `System\Models\File` record.

### Impact

An authenticated attacker could view, modify, or reorder attachment metadata (e.g., file names, descriptions, sort order) for files uploaded by other users or associated with different records, leading to unauthorized data access and manipulation.

### Key diff

```
--- a/modules/backend/formwidgets/FileUpload.php
+++ b/modules/backend/formwidgets/FileUpload.php
@@ -175,14 +177,21 @@ protected function prepareVars()
     protected function getFileRecord()
     {
         $record = false;
-
         if (!empty(post('file_id'))) {
-            $record = $this->getRelationModel()->find(post('file_id')) ?: false;
+            $record = $this->getRelationObject()
+                ->withDeferred($this->sessionKey)
+                ->find(post('file_id')) ?: false;
         }
-
         return $record;
     }
 
@@ -351,11 +360,25 @@ public function onRemoveAttachment(): void
     public function onSortAttachments(): void
     {
         if ($sortData = post('sortOrder')) {
+            $keyName = $this->getRelationModel()->getKeyName();
+            $validIds = $this->getRelationObject()
+                ->withDeferred($this->sessionKey)
+                ->pluck($keyName)
+                ->all();
+
+            $sortData = array_intersect_key($sortData, array_flip($validIds));
+            if (empty($sortData)) {
+                return;
+            }
+
             $ids = array_keys($sortData);
             $orders = array_values($sortData);
-
-            $fileModel = $this->getRelationModel();
-            $fileModel->setSortableOrder($ids, $orders);
+            $this->getRelationModel()->setSortableOrder($ids, $orders);
         }
     }
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a scope to the file lookup and sorting operations. When retrieving or sorting files, the system now explicitly checks if the `file_id` belongs to the current widget's relation, including any files bound via the current deferred-binding session. This prevents an attacker from referencing arbitrary `System\Models\File` records.

## GHSA-fm29-4mq3-phg6 · winter/wn-backend-module

2026-08-20 · PHP · **MODERATE** 5.3 · Pattern: `MISSING_AUTHZ→RESOURCE`

### Root cause

The AJAX handlers for import and export operations within the ImportExportController behavior did not perform granular permission checks. While the controller itself might have had general access control, the specific AJAX methods (e.g., onImport, onExport, download) lacked explicit checks to ensure the authenticated user had the necessary 'import' or 'export' permissions.

### Impact

An authenticated backend user, even without specific import or export permissions, could bypass the intended access controls and perform import or export operations, potentially leading to unauthorized data manipulation or exfiltration.

### Key diff

```
--- a/modules/backend/behaviors/ImportExportController.php
+++ b/modules/backend/behaviors/ImportExportController.php
@@ -181,6 +184,10 @@ public function export()
 
     public function download($name, $outputName = null)
     {
+        if (!$this->userHasAccess('export')) {
+            abort(403);
+        }
+
         $this->controller->pageTitle = $this->controller->pageTitle
             ?: Lang::get($this->getConfig('export[title]', 'Export records'));
 
@@ -193,6 +200,10 @@ public function download($name, $outputName = null)
 
     public function onImport()
     {
+        if (!$this->userHasAccess('import')) {
+            abort(403);
+        }
+
         try {
             $model = $this->importGetModel();
             $matches = post('column_match', []);
```

### Fix summary

The patch adds explicit permission checks to all relevant AJAX handler methods (download, onImport, onImportLoadForm, onImportLoadColumnSampleForm, onExport, onExportLoadForm). Before executing the core logic, each method now calls `userHasAccess('import')` or `userHasAccess('export')` and aborts with a 403 Forbidden error if the user lacks the required permission.

## GHSA-r553-m4fv-5v97 · github.com/axllent/mailpit

2026-08-20 · Go · **MODERATE** 5.3 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

The application previously read an entire line from the SMTP DATA stream into memory using `s.br.ReadBytes('\n')` before enforcing the maximum message size. If a malicious client sent a single very long line without a newline character, the buffer could grow beyond the intended limit, consuming excessive memory.

### Impact

An attacker could send a specially crafted, oversized SMTP DATA line to the server, causing it to allocate a large amount of memory. This could lead to a denial of service by exhausting available memory resources or crashing the server.

### Key diff

```
- line, err := s.br.ReadBytes('\n')
- if err != nil {
-  return nil, err
+ var line []byte
+ for {
+  fragment, err := s.br.ReadSlice('\n')
+  line = append(line, fragment...)
+  if err == nil {
+   break
+  }
+  if err != bufio.ErrBufferFull {
+   return nil, err
+  }
+  if s.srv.MaxSize > 0 && len(data)+len(line) > s.srv.MaxSize {
+   _, _ = s.br.Discard(s.br.Buffered())
+   return nil, maxSizeExceeded(s.srv.MaxSize)
+  }
 }
```

### Fix summary

The patch modifies the `readData` function to read SMTP DATA lines in buffer-sized chunks using `s.br.ReadSlice('\n')`. It now checks the total message size against `s.srv.MaxSize` proactively whenever the internal buffer fills up without encountering a newline, preventing over-allocation before the full line is received.

## GHSA-2223-f22x-24cq · winter/wn-system-module

2026-08-20 · PHP · **MODERATE** 4.9 · Pattern: `PATH_TRAVERSAL→FILE_READ`

### Root cause

The JavascriptImporter allowed the `=include` directive to resolve arbitrary file paths through `realpath()` traversal without sufficient path confinement. An attacker could use path traversal sequences (e.g., `../../../`) to escape the intended directory and include sensitive files from the server's filesystem into the publicly accessible compiled JavaScript output.

### Impact

An attacker could read arbitrary files on the server that the web process has read access to, potentially leading to the disclosure of sensitive information like environment variables, configuration files, or source code.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/Parse/Assetic/Filter/JavascriptImporter.php
+++ b/src/Parse/Assetic/Filter/JavascriptImporter.php
@@ -106,6 +106,23 @@ protected function directiveInclude($data, $required = false)
                 continue;
             }
 
+            if (!PathResolver::withinAny($scriptPath, $allowedRoots)) {
+                $errorMsg = sprintf("File '%s' is outside the allowed import paths. in %s", $script, $this->scriptFile);
+                if ($required) {
+                    throw new RuntimeException($errorMsg);
+                }
+
+                $result .= '/* ' . $errorMsg . ' */' . PHP_EOL;
+                continue;
+            }
+
             /*
              * Exclude duplicates
              */
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a `HasAllowedImportRoots` trait and a `PathResolver::withinAny()` method to strictly confine included files to the originating script's directory subtree or explicitly allowed roots. It also adds a check to ensure only `.js` files can be included, preventing the inclusion of non-JavaScript files even if path confinement is bypassed.

## GHSA-58fp-mcx6-7qf9 · winter/wn-backend-module

2026-08-20 · PHP · **MODERATE** 4.9 · Pattern: `PATH_TRAVERSAL→FILE_READ`

### Root cause

The `Less_Parser` in `wikimedia/less.php`, when processing `@import` directives, had a fallback mechanism that would read an attacker-supplied raw path using `file_get_contents()` if no candidate import directory matched. This allowed an attacker to specify arbitrary file paths via `@import (inline)` in untrusted CSS, leading to file disclosure.

### Impact

An attacker could read arbitrary server-readable files on the system by crafting malicious LESS stylesheets, potentially disclosing sensitive information.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/Parse/Assetic/Filter/LessCompiler.php
+++ b/src/Parse/Assetic/Filter/LessCompiler.php
@@ -45,7 +45,12 @@
         // CSS Rewriter will take care of this
         $parser->SetOption('relativeUrls', false);
 
-        $parser->parseFile($asset->getSourceRoot() . '/' . $asset->getSourcePath());
+        $sourceFile = $asset->getSourceRoot() . '/' . $asset->getSourcePath();
+
+        $parser->SetImportDirs(
+            LessImportResolver::buildImportDirs($sourceFile, $this->allowedImportRoots)
+        );
+
+        $parser->parseFile($sourceFile);
 
         // Set the LESS variables after parsing to override them
         $parser->ModifyVars($this->presets);
```

### Fix summary

A new `LessImportResolver` class was introduced to strictly control `@import` resolution. It builds a custom resolver closure for `Less_Parser::SetImportDirs()` that validates imported file paths against a list of allowed roots (the source file's directory and caller-configured roots). Any import outside these allowed paths is redirected to an empty sentinel file, preventing arbitrary file disclosure.

## GHSA-x3g7-qrwc-f6c5 · github.com/alexandre-daubois/ember

2026-08-20 · Go · **MODERATE** 4.7 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→LOG`

### Root cause

The application was displaying log entries and other attacker-controlled strings directly to the operator's terminal without sanitizing them. This allowed malicious input containing ANSI/OSC/CSI escape sequences to be interpreted by the terminal.

### Impact

An attacker could inject terminal escape sequences into the operator's terminal, potentially leading to defacement of the terminal, execution of arbitrary commands (if the terminal emulator supports it), or other undesirable behavior for the operator.

### Key diff

```
--- a/internal/ui/logs.go
+++ b/internal/ui/logs.go
@@ -214,7 +214,9 @@ func (a *App) logsEmptyHint(visibleCount int) string {
 		return "No matching log lines (filter: " + a.filter + ")"
 	}
 	if sourceLen > 0 && a.logSel.kind == logSelAccessHost {
-		return "No access logs yet for " + a.logSel.host
+		// logSel.host is an access-log host name (attacker-controlled) echoed
+		// outside the fitCellLeft path, so neutralise it here.
+		return "No access logs yet for " + sanitizeControl(a.logSel.host)
 	}
 	if a.logSource != "" {
 		return "Listening on " + a.logSource + " — waiting for log lines (it can take up to 30s)..."
```

### Fix summary

A new `sanitizeControl` function was introduced to filter out C0 and C1 control characters (including ESC, CSI, OSC) from strings before they are rendered to the terminal. This function is now applied to all potentially attacker-controlled inputs displayed in the TUI, such as log hostnames, error messages, and log table cells.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 2 occurrences**
> Languages: Go, JavaScript
> → GHSA-8h6h-x5pq-56fq

## GHSA-5cwr-5jxg-pcf6 · winter/wn-backend-module

2026-08-20 · PHP · **MODERATE** 4.5 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→XSS`

### Root cause

The application cached custom CSS content from Brand Settings and Editor Settings without proper sanitization. When this cached content was later retrieved and rendered, it was directly outputted to the browser, allowing for the execution of malicious scripts if an attacker could inject them into the custom CSS.

### Impact

An attacker with the ability to modify Brand Settings or Editor Settings (e.g., a privileged backend user) could inject malicious JavaScript. This script would then execute in the browsers of other backend users who view pages where this CSS is rendered, leading to session hijacking, data theft, or further compromise of the backend.

### Key diff

```
--- a/modules/backend/models/BrandSetting.php
+++ b/modules/backend/models/BrandSetting.php
@@ -185,7 +185,7 @@ public static function renderCss()
     {
         $cacheKey = self::instance()->cacheKey;
         if (Cache::has($cacheKey)) {
-            return Cache::get($cacheKey);
+            return strip_tags(Cache::get($cacheKey));
         }
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces `strip_tags()` to the cached CSS content before it is returned and rendered in `BrandSetting.php` and `EditorSetting.php`. This ensures that any HTML or script tags injected into the custom CSS are removed, preventing XSS. Additionally, comments were added to relevant permissions to highlight the security risk associated with managing these settings.

## GHSA-hq84-x37p-j6q5 · winter/wn-backend-module

2026-08-20 · PHP · **MODERATE** 4.5 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→XSS`

### Root cause

The application was directly outputting user-supplied input from the 'search' GET parameter into an HTML attribute without proper escaping. This allowed an attacker to inject malicious scripts into the HTML.

### Impact

An attacker could inject arbitrary client-side scripts into the web page, leading to session hijacking, defacement, or redirection to malicious sites for users viewing the affected page.

### Key diff

```
-                value="<?= get('search') ?>"
+                value="<?= e(get('search')); ?>"
```

### Fix summary

The patch adds an HTML entity encoding function, `e()`, around the output of the `get('search')` parameter. This ensures that any special HTML characters in the user input are properly escaped before being rendered in the HTML attribute.

## GHSA-hvq6-2r72-p2x7 · django-cms

2026-08-20 · Python · **MODERATE** 4.4 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→XSS`

### Root cause

The vulnerability existed because an exception message, which could contain user-controlled input, was directly embedded into an HTML heading tag without proper escaping in the `render_exception` function. This allowed an attacker to inject malicious scripts into the rendered error page.

### Impact

An attacker could inject arbitrary client-side scripts into the Django CMS edit-mode interface. This could lead to session hijacking, defacement, or redirection of legitimate users.

### Key diff

```
--- a/cms/plugin_rendering.py
+++ b/cms/plugin_rendering.py
@@ -598,7 +599,7 @@ def render_exception(
                 html = reporter.get_traceback_html()
             else:
                 html = ""
-            heading = f'<h2 class="cms-rendering-exception-title">{message}</h2>'
+            heading = format_html('<h2 class="cms-rendering-exception-title">{}</h2>', message)
```

### Fix summary

The patch addresses the XSS vulnerability by using `django.utils.html.format_html` to properly escape the `message` variable before embedding it into the HTML heading. This ensures that any special characters in the message are converted to their HTML entities, preventing script injection. Additionally, a CSRF protection was added to the `clear_placeholder` endpoint by requiring POST requests.

## GHSA-8qj2-c6q4-f399 · django-cms

2026-08-20 · Python · **MODERATE** 4.3 · Pattern: `MISSING_AUTHZ→RESOURCE`

### Root cause

The `render_object_structure` view, which displays the structure board for frontend-editable objects, did not properly check for view permissions on non-PageContent objects. It only checked for `cms.use_structure` permission, allowing low-privileged staff users to view the placeholder structure of objects they were not authorized to see.

### Impact

Low-privileged staff users could gain unauthorized access to the structural information of content objects (e.g., plugins, placeholders) that they should not have been able to view, potentially leading to information disclosure.

### Key diff

```
--- a/cms/views.py
+++ b/cms/views.py
@@ -264,6 +265,11 @@ def render_object_structure(request, content_type_id, object_id):
                 raise Http404
         else:
             content_type_obj = content_type.get_object_for_this_type(pk=object_id)
+            # Viewing the structure board requires only view permission (as the
+            # page branch above does via user_can_view_page). Mutations remain
+            # gated by change permission at the plugin endpoints.
+            if not user_can_view_placeholder_source(request.user, content_type_obj):
+                raise Http404
     except ObjectDoesNotExist as err:
         raise Http404 from err
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a new utility function `user_can_view_placeholder_source` to consistently check for view permissions on the source object of a placeholder. This function is now used in `render_object_structure` and `_can_add_structure_mode` to ensure that users have explicit view or change permissions before accessing the structure board for any object type.

## GHSA-mpmw-f6h6-3g26 · winter/wn-backend-module

2026-08-20 · PHP · **MODERATE** 4.3 · Pattern: `IDOR→DATA_ACCESS`

### Root cause

The 'My Account' preview functionality in the Winter CMS backend allowed a user to view any backend user's profile by manipulating the record ID in the URL. The controller did not restrict the queried user record to the currently authenticated user, enabling unauthorized data access.

### Impact

An authenticated backend user could view the profile details of any other backend user, potentially exposing sensitive information like email addresses, names, or other profile data.

### Key diff

```
--- a/modules/backend/controllers/MyAccount.php
+++ b/modules/backend/controllers/MyAccount.php
@@ -51,6 +57,11 @@ public function __construct()
         SettingsManager::setContext('Winter.Backend', 'myaccount');
     }
 
+    public function formExtendQuery(Builder $query): void
+    {
+        $query->whereKey($this->user->getKey());
+    }
+
     /**
      * My Account page
      */
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a `formExtendQuery` method to the `MyAccount` controller. This method explicitly filters the database query to ensure that only the record associated with the currently authenticated user's key can be retrieved, preventing access to other users' profiles.

## GHSA-2p39-2jf3-fv2q · next-video

2026-08-20 · JavaScript · **MODERATE** 0.0 · Pattern: `PATH_TRAVERSAL→FILE_READ`

### Root cause

The application allowed unauthenticated users to request video assets via the `/api/video` handler. The `url` parameter in these requests was not properly validated to ensure it pointed to a file within the intended video folder, allowing path traversal sequences (e.g., `../`) to access arbitrary files outside the configured directory.

### Impact

An attacker could read any file on the server's filesystem that the application had read permissions for, potentially leading to the disclosure of sensitive information such as configuration files, source code, or user data.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/request-handler.ts
+++ b/src/request-handler.ts
@@ -41,6 +41,12 @@ async function getRequest(url?: string | null) {
     };
   }
 
+  try {
+    await assertSafeAssetSource(url);
+  } catch {
+    return { status: 404, data: { error: 'asset not found' } };
+  }
+
   let asset;
   try {
     asset = await getAsset(url);
```

### Fix summary

A new function `assertSafeAssetSource` was introduced to validate asset URLs. This function checks if the requested `url` resolves within the configured video `folder` and throws an error if it attempts to traverse outside. This validation is now applied to both `GET` and `POST` requests in the `request-handler.ts` before processing the asset.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 75 occurrences**

## GHSA-f4jp-rw7w-ccwg · gettext-converter

2026-08-20 · JavaScript · **MODERATE** 0.0 · Pattern: `PROTOTYPE_POLLUTION→OVERRIDE`

### Root cause

The `js2i18next` function in the `gettext-converter` library did not properly sanitize or validate translation keys. Specifically, when processing translation objects, it used bracket notation to assign values based on potentially attacker-controlled keys, allowing an attacker to inject properties into `Object.prototype`.

### Impact

An attacker could inject arbitrary properties into `Object.prototype`, which could lead to various issues such as denial of service, remote code execution in specific contexts, or property overwrites in other parts of the application that rely on object properties.

## GHSA-ghvf-qf6h-g8x5 · @nocobase/server

2026-08-20 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 0.0 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→COMMAND`

### Root cause

The application allowed unvalidated user input to be used in plugin names, which were then used in CLI commands for plugin management. This lack of validation, combined with the ability to specify arbitrary package names, could lead to an attacker writing files to arbitrary locations and subsequently achieving remote code execution through a local file inclusion vulnerability.

### Impact

An attacker could achieve remote code execution on the server by crafting malicious plugin names that lead to arbitrary file writes, followed by exploiting a local file inclusion vulnerability to execute the written malicious code.

### Key diff

```
--- a/packages/core/server/src/plugin-manager/options/resource.ts
+++ b/packages/core/server/src/plugin-manager/options/resource.ts
@@ -14,7 +14,7 @@ import fse from 'fs-extra';
 import path from 'path';
 import Application from '../../application';
 import PluginManager from '../plugin-manager';
-import { pmListSummary } from '../utils';
+import { assertSafePluginPackageName, pmListSummary } from '../utils';
 
 import packageJson from '../../../package.json';
 
@@ -314,12 +314,19 @@ export default {
       if (!keys.length) {
         ctx.throw(400, 'plugin name invalid');
       }
+      for (const key of keys) {
+        try {
+          assertSafePluginPackageName(key);
+        } catch (error) {
+          ctx.throw(400, 'plugin name invalid');
+        }
+      }
       const awaitResponse = coerceAwaitResponse(awaitResponseRaw);
       const argv = ['pm', 'enable', ...keys];
       if (awaitResponse) {
         await app.runAsCLI(argv, { from: 'user', throwError: true });
       } else {
-        void app.runAsCLI(argv, { from: 'user' }).catch((err) => {
+        app.runAsCLI(argv, { from: 'user' }).catch((err) => {
           app.log.error(err);
         });
       }
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a new function, `assertSafePluginPackageName`, which validates plugin names to ensure they do not contain path traversal characters or other unsafe constructs. This function is now called before processing any plugin names in CLI commands, preventing malicious input from being used to write arbitrary files.

## GHSA-v667-gc2r-2xm7 · @whyour/qinglong

2026-08-20 · JavaScript · **CRITICAL** 0.0 · Pattern: `MISSING_AUTH→ENDPOINT`

### Root cause

The previous fix for CVE-2026-3965 was incomplete. It only covered specific API endpoints for user initialization, but overlooked alternative paths that could also be used to initialize a user without proper authentication checks.

### Impact

An attacker could potentially initialize a user account or notification settings without being authenticated, which might lead to unauthorized access or control over the application's initial setup.

### Key diff

```
--- a/back/loaders/express.ts
+++ b/back/loaders/express.ts
@@ -120,7 +120,14 @@ export default ({ app }: { app: Application }) => {
   app.use(async (req, res, next) => {
     const pathLower = req.path.toLowerCase();
-    if (!['/api/user/init', '/api/user/notification/init'].includes(pathLower)) {
+    if (
+      ![
+        '/api/user/init',
+        '/api/user/notification/init',
+        '/open/user/init',
+        '/open/user/notification/init',
+      ].includes(req.path)
+    ) {
       return next();
     }
     const authInfo =
```

### Fix summary

The patch extends the list of unauthenticated paths to include '/open/user/init' and '/open/user/notification/init'. This ensures that all known user initialization endpoints are properly excluded from the authentication middleware, preventing an incomplete authentication bypass.
